      # D-0060 workspace-refactor recovery

      Status: **recovered; production resumed from validated per-task outputs**.

      ## Event

      - `[OBSERVED]` At approximately 2026-08-24 17:35 UTC, an external workspace
        refactor moved the pre-refactor research tree from `/jackbox/home/MH370` to
        `/jackbox/home/.iso/thread-storage/MH370-legacy-pre-refactor-20260824` while
        the D-0060 rare-event matrix was running.
      - `[OBSERVED]` The archive contained 415 complete grouped weighted-ensemble
        tasks. Four in-flight workers then failed because their source path had moved;
        they produced no accepted result and were rerun from deterministic seeds.
      - `[OBSERVED]` The obsolete matrix driver remained alive after failure because
        its pending/failed loop did not terminate. Only the exact obsolete supervisor,
        matrix-driver, and old-watcher PIDs were sent `SIGTERM`; no result file was
        deleted.

      ## Recovery controls

      - `[DERIVED]` Split-output recovery used `cp -an`, so no archived file could be
        overwritten. The resumed archive-resident matrix independently classified
        exactly 415/885 groups as complete using metadata identity, configuration and
        target hashes, replicate-row counts, and nonempty resampling diagnostics.
      - `[DERIVED]` The resumed production driver independently classified all 72/72
        ordinary tasks as complete before rebuilding derived surfaces.
      - `[DERIVED]` The strengthened regression suite passed 53/53 tests after recovery.
      - `[DERIVED]` All four matrix drivers were patched to exit after active workers
        drain when a failure has occurred, preventing the observed infinite loop.

      ## Autonomous continuation

      - `[DERIVED]` `src/run_until_admitted_domain.py` waits for the exact cached
        pre-ultra pipeline PID before starting a new pipeline and refuses concurrent
        completion drivers.
      - `[DERIVED]` It may mutate the source grid only through the frozen
        `prepare_cross_arc_expansion.py` decision, only when the final debris audit
        requires cross-arc expansion and does not require along-arc expansion.
      - `[DERIVED]` Each cycle and command log is recorded under `outputs/`.

      - `[DERIVED]` Before a permitted expansion mutates the grid, every direct input
        and production artifact is copied to an iteration-specific archive and
        verified by SHA-256. Existing numerical outputs may be reused only when the
        executable reuse check proves byte identity, a config change limited to the
        production offset list, exact preservation of the relevant source rows, and
        exact equality of any applicable target rows. The rebound metadata retains
        the predecessor hashes, and the independent final audit rehashes every reuse
        record; a failed proof falls back to deterministic recomputation.

      - `[UNKNOWN]` The original top-level orchestration state and the long first-run
        matrix progress log were not preserved before new stage-numbered logs replaced
        them. This loses timing/progress history, not the per-task scientific outputs:
        ordinary and weighted-ensemble CSVs retain their companion metadata, seeds or
        replicate identities, resampling diagnostics, and input hashes. The final input
        and output manifests are rooted at the archive path and hash those retained
        artifacts.

      This recovery changes storage location and orchestration only. It does not alter
      any drift, leeway, beaching, detection, reporting, isotope, or prior assumption.

      from __future__ import annotations

      import json
      import sys
      import tempfile
      import unittest
      from pathlib import Path
      from unittest import mock

      import numpy as np
      import pandas as pd


      ANALYSIS = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
      SRC = ANALYSIS / "src"
      sys.path.insert(0, str(SRC))
      from audit_final_deliverables import pdf_page_count, png_dimensions  # noqa: E402

      class CompletionPipelineTests(unittest.TestCase):
          def test_pipeline_contains_full_frozen_stage_order(self) -> None:
              steps = pipeline_steps("/analysis/python", ANALYSIS)
              names = [name for name, _ in steps]
              self.assertEqual(
                  names,
                  [
                      "production_matrix",
                      "regression_tests",
                      "ordinary_surfaces",
                      "zero_offset_anchor_selection",
                      "rare_target_selection",
                      "rare_event_matrix",
                      "rare_refinement_rank10_selection",
                      "rare_refinement_rank10_matrix",
                      "rare_refinement_rank5_selection",
                      "rare_refinement_rank5_matrix",
                      "rare_linear_predecessor_audit",
                      "rare_rank5_log_replay",
                      "rare_strong_log_selection",
                      "rare_strong_log_matrix",
                      "rare_ultra_log_selection",
                      "rare_ultra_log_matrix",
                      "rare_log_final_audit",
                      "final_event_composition",
                      "rare_log_surface_convergence",
                      "ordinary_composed_surfaces",
                      "isotope_screen_selection",
                      "isotope_screen_matrix",
                      "isotope_screen_summary",
                      "isotope_screen_surfaces",
                      "isotope_final_selection",
                      "isotope_final_matrix",
                      "isotope_final_summary",
                      "final_surfaces",
                      "admission_audit",
                      "integration_export",
                      "render_charts",
                      "result_summary",
                      "northern_branch_diagnostic",
                      "independent_final_audit",
                      "build_manifests",
                  ],
              )
              self.assertTrue(all(command[0] == "/analysis/python" for _, command in steps))
              self.assertLess(names.index("admission_audit"), names.index("integration_export"))
              self.assertLess(names.index("integration_export"), names.index("render_charts"))
              self.assertEqual(names[-1], "build_manifests")


          def test_reference_bma_guard_uses_common_scale_raw_likelihoods(self) -> None:
              rows = [
                  {
                      "family": family,
                      "likelihood_variant": "debris_plus_isotope_central",
                      "source_cell_id": f"s{index:03d}",
                      "raw_log_likelihood": value,
                  }
                  for family, value in (("hycom", 0.0), ("gdp", np.log(3.0)))
                  for index in range(225)
              ]
              frame = pd.DataFrame(rows)
              combined = reference_bma_frame(
                  frame, ("hycom", "gdp"), np.asarray([0.5, 0.5])
              )
              self.assertEqual(len(combined), 225)
              self.assertTrue(
                  np.allclose(
                      combined["raw_log_likelihood"].to_numpy(float),
                      np.log(2.0),
                  )
              )
              self.assertTrue(
                  np.equal(combined["relative_log_likelihood"], 0.0).all()
              )
              with self.assertRaises(AssertionError):
                  reference_bma_frame(
                      frame.iloc[:-1], ("hycom", "gdp"), np.asarray([0.5, 0.5])
                  )

          def test_ordinary_resume_requires_declared_companion_tables(self) -> None:
              with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as directory:
                  root = Path(directory)
                  metadata = root / "metadata_hycom_flaperon_central.json"
                  scores = root / "scores_hycom_flaperon_central.csv"
                  isotope = root / "isotope_hycom_flaperon_central.csv"
                  payload = {
                      "stage": "production",
                      "family": "hycom",
                      "cross_arc_offset_nm": 0.0,
                      "coast_case": "central",
                      "detection_case_exported": "central",
                      "particles_per_cell": 16384,
                      "ordinary_output_rows": 1,
                      "isotope_output_rows": 1,
                      "object_class": "flaperon",
                      "source_grid_sha256": "gridhash",
                      "alpha_or_pseudocount": 0.0,
                  }
                  ordinary_identity = {
                      "stage": "production",
                      "family": "hycom",
                      "object_class": "flaperon",
                      "offset_nm": 0.0,
                  }
                  metadata.write_text(json.dumps(payload), encoding="utf-8")
                  scores.write_text("field\nvalue\n", encoding="utf-8")
                  self.assertFalse(is_complete(metadata, 16384, "gridhash", **ordinary_identity))
                  isotope.write_text("field\nvalue\n", encoding="utf-8")
                  self.assertTrue(is_complete(metadata, 16384, "gridhash", **ordinary_identity))
                  self.assertFalse(
                      is_complete(metadata, 16384, "gridhash", **{**ordinary_identity, "family": "gdp"})
                  )
                  scores.write_text("field\n", encoding="utf-8")
                  self.assertFalse(is_complete(metadata, 16384, "gridhash", **ordinary_identity))
                  scores.write_text("field\nvalue\n", encoding="utf-8")
                  self.assertFalse(is_complete(metadata, 16384, "other-grid", **ordinary_identity))

          def test_weighted_resume_requires_hashes_and_replicate_artifacts(self) -> None:
              with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as directory:
                  root = Path(directory)
                  rare = root / "grouped_hycom_flaperon_fixture.json"
                  rare_payload = {
                      "family": "hycom",
                      "object_class": "flaperon",
                      "source_cell_id": "fixture",
                      "particles_per_replicate": 4096,
                      "replicates": 2,
                      "target_count": 1,
                      "config_sha256": "config",
                      "target_matrix_sha256": "targets",
                      "alpha_or_pseudocount": 0.0,
                  }
                  rare_identity = {
                      "family": "hycom",
                      "object_class": "flaperon",
                      "source_cell_id": "fixture",
                  }
                  rare.write_text(json.dumps(rare_payload), encoding="utf-8")
                  rare.with_suffix(".csv").write_text(
                      "replicate\n0\n1\n", encoding="utf-8"
                  )
                  rare.with_name(rare.stem + "_resampling.csv").write_text(
                      "day\n30\n", encoding="utf-8"
                  )

                      rare_complete(rare, 4096, 2, "config", "targets", **rare_identity)

                      rare_complete(rare, 4096, 2, "config", "targets", **rare_identity)

                  self.assertFalse(
                      rare_complete(rare, 4096, 2, "config", "other-targets", **rare_identity)
                  )

      ) -> pd.DataFrame:

      #!/usr/bin/env python3
      """Run one independent event-specific ultra log-domain refinement."""

      from __future__ import annotations

      import argparse
      import json
      import math
      import sys
      from pathlib import Path

      import numpy as np
      import pandas as pd

      from file_integrity import sha256
      from log_rare_refinement_core import one_group_log_replicate


      REPO = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[3]
      D0045_SRC = REPO / "analyses" / "D-0045-rare-event-debris-likelihood" / "src"
      sys.path.insert(0, str(D0045_SRC))
      from run_target import build_fields, load_target, stable_seed  # noqa: E402


      def ultra_event_stem(
          family: str, object_class: str, source_cell_id: str, record_id: str
      ) -> str:
          return f"ultra_{family}_{object_class}_{source_cell_id}_{record_id}"


      def main() -> None:
          parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
          parser.add_argument("--family", choices=("hycom", "gdp"), required=True)
          parser.add_argument("--object-class", required=True)
          parser.add_argument("--source-cell-id", required=True)
          parser.add_argument("--record-id", required=True)
          parser.add_argument("--quiet", action="store_true")
          args = parser.parse_args()

          analysis = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
          inputs = analysis / "inputs"
          config_path = inputs / "config.json"
          repair_path = inputs / "log_domain_rare_repair.json"
          ultra_path = inputs / "ultra_log_rare_refinement.json"
          target_path = inputs / "rare_event_hybrid_ultra_target_matrix.csv"
          config = json.loads(config_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
          repair = json.loads(repair_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
          ultra = json.loads(ultra_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
          if ultra.get("schema") != "d0060-ultra-log-rare-refinement-v1":
              raise AssertionError("Unexpected ultra-refinement specification")
          specification = ultra["ultra_material_refinement"]
          matrix = pd.read_csv(target_path)
          selected = matrix[
              (matrix["family"] == args.family)
              & (matrix["object_class"] == args.object_class)
              & (matrix["source_cell_id"] == args.source_cell_id)
              & (matrix["record_id"].astype(str) == str(args.record_id))
          ]
          if len(selected) != 1:
              raise ValueError("Ultra log refinement key must select exactly one row")
          row = selected.iloc[0]
          release = (float(row["source_latitude"]), float(row["source_longitude"]))
          target = load_target(analysis, args.object_class, str(args.record_id))
          fields = build_fields(analysis, args.family)[:4]
          particles = int(specification["particles_per_replicate"])
          replicates = int(specification["replicates"])
          output_rows: list[dict[str, object]] = []
          diagnostics: list[dict[str, object]] = []
          for replicate in range(replicates):

      quality_function
          """Evaluate replicate quality without exponentiating the absolute scale."""
          values = np.asarray(log_values, dtype=float)
          if values.ndim != 1 or len(values) < 2:
              raise ValueError("At least two one-dimensional replicates are required")
          if np.isnan(values).any() or np.isposinf(values).any() or np.any(values > 1e-12):
              raise ValueError("Log recovery probabilities must lie in [-inf, 0]")
          finite = np.isfinite(values)
          finite_count = int(np.count_nonzero(finite))
          if finite_count:
              maximum = float(np.max(values[finite]))
              scaled = np.zeros(len(values), dtype=float)
              scaled[finite] = np.exp(values[finite] - maximum)
              scaled_mean = float(scaled.mean())
              log_mean = maximum + math.log(scaled_mean)
              coefficient = float(scaled.std(ddof=1) / scaled_mean)
              log_standard_error = (
                  log_mean + math.log(coefficient) - 0.5 * math.log(len(values))
                  if coefficient > 0.0
                  else -math.inf
              )
          else:
              log_mean = -math.inf
              coefficient = math.inf
              log_standard_error = -math.inf
          passes = bool(
              finite_count >= minimum_finite_replicates
              and coefficient <= maximum_coefficient_of_variation
          )
          reasons: list[str] = []
          if finite_count < minimum_finite_replicates:
              reasons.append("too_few_finite_log_replicates")
          if coefficient > maximum_coefficient_of_variation:
              reasons.append("replicate_cv_above_limit")
          return {
              "log_replicate_mean": log_mean,
              "replicate_mean_linear_diagnostic": (
                  float(math.exp(log_mean))
                  if log_mean >= LOG_SMALLEST_SUBNORMAL
                  else 0.0
              ),
              "log_replicate_standard_error": log_standard_error,
              "replicate_standard_error_linear_diagnostic": (
                  float(math.exp(log_standard_error))
                  if log_standard_error >= LOG_SMALLEST_SUBNORMAL
                  else 0.0
              ),
              "replicate_coefficient_of_variation": coefficient,
              "finite_log_replicates": finite_count,
              "negative_infinity_replicates": int(len(values) - finite_count),
              "linear_mean_underflows": bool(log_mean < LOG_SMALLEST_SUBNORMAL),
              "passes": passes,
              "failure_reasons": ";".join(reasons),
          }


      def one_group_log_replicate(
          *,
          object_class: str,
          release: tuple[float, float],
          targets: list[dict[str, object]],
          fields,
          particles: int,
          seed: int,
          coast_case: str,
          detection_case: str,
          resampling_days: int,
          rank_bins: int,
          fixed_distance_boundaries_km: np.ndarray | None = None,
      ) -> tuple[np.ndarray, list[dict[str, object]]]:
          """Estimate grouped first-detection probabilities in stable log space."""
          if particles < 1 or not targets:
              raise ValueError("Positive particles and at least one target are required")
          if fixed_distance_boundaries_km is not None and len(targets) != 1:
              raise ValueError("Hybrid fixed-distance mode is event-specific")
          current, wind, stokes, coast = fields
          rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

          longitude = np.full(particles, release[1], dtype=float)
          beached = np.zeros(particles, dtype=bool)
          lost = np.zeros(particles, dtype=bool)
          parameters = sample_object_parameters(object_class, (particles,), rng)
          log_survival = np.zeros((len(targets), particles), dtype=float)
          log_detection = np.full((len(targets), particles), -np.inf, dtype=float)
          weights = np.full(particles, 1.0 / particles, dtype=float)
          terminal_log_detection = np.full(len(targets), -np.inf, dtype=float)
          diagnostics: list[dict[str, object]] = []

          final_date = max(target["date_end_value"] for target in targets)

                  log_detection = log_detection[:, source].copy()

                          "effective_sample_size_after": result.effective_sample_size_after,
                          "active_probability_weight": float(weights.sum()),
                          "beached_fraction_after": float(np.mean(beached)),
                          "minimum_active_target_distance_km": float(distances.min()),
                          "terminal_finite_log_target_count": int(
                              np.isfinite(terminal_log_detection).sum()
                          ),
                      }
                  )
          estimates = terminal_log_detection.copy()
          if len(weights):
              current_terms = log_detection + np.log(weights)[None, :]
              estimates = np.logaddexp(estimates, logsumexp(current_terms, axis=1))

      """Log-domain weighted-ensemble repair for ultra-rare D-0060 recoveries.

      The declared recovery-location kernel is Gaussian and therefore strictly
      positive at every finite distance.  Its binary64 evaluation nevertheless
      underflows at large distances.  These helpers retain first-detection survival
      and detection mass in log space, without adding an alpha or probability floor.
      """

      from __future__ import annotations

      import math
      import sys
      from datetime import date, timedelta
      from pathlib import Path

      import numpy as np


      REPO = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[3]
      D0045_SRC = REPO / "analyses" / "D-0045-rare-event-debris-likelihood" / "src"
      sys.path.insert(0, str(D0045_SRC))
      from run_target import (  # noqa: E402
          COAST_CASES,
          DETECTION_CASES,
          RELEASE_DATE,
          d42core,
          sample_object_parameters,
          step,
      )
      from weighted_ensemble_core import stratified_resample  # noqa: E402

      from rare_refinement_core import (  # noqa: E402
          equal_count_rank_labels,
          target_beach_rank_strata,
      )


      RECOVERY_BANDWIDTH_KM = 100.0
      LOG_SMALLEST_SUBNORMAL = math.log(float(np.nextafter(0.0, 1.0)))


      def logsumexp(values: np.ndarray, axis: int | None = None) -> np.ndarray:
          """Stable log-sum-exp that preserves all-negative-infinity slices."""
          array = np.asarray(values, dtype=float)
          maximum = np.max(array, axis=axis, keepdims=True)
          finite = np.isfinite(maximum)
          with np.errstate(invalid="ignore", divide="ignore"):
              shifted = np.where(finite, array - maximum, -np.inf)
              total = np.sum(np.exp(shifted), axis=axis, keepdims=True)
              result = np.where(finite, maximum + np.log(total), -np.inf)
          if axis is None:
              return np.asarray(result.squeeze())
          return np.squeeze(result, axis=axis)


      def log_hazard_from_log_rate(log_rate: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
          """Return ``log(1-exp(-exp(log_rate)))`` without tail underflow."""
          log_rate = np.asarray(log_rate, dtype=float)
          if np.isnan(log_rate).any() or np.isposinf(log_rate).any():
              raise ValueError("Log rates must be finite or negative infinity")
          output = np.empty(log_rate.shape, dtype=float)
          tiny = log_rate < -36.0
          # At this threshold the omitted relative correction is below binary64
          # precision, while retaining log_rate avoids exp(log_rate) underflow.
          output[tiny] = log_rate[tiny]
          regular = ~tiny
          if np.any(regular):
              rate = np.exp(log_rate[regular])
              output[regular] = np.log(-np.expm1(-rate))
          return output


      def score_target_day_log(
          when: date,
          latitude: np.ndarray,
          longitude: np.ndarray,
          beached: np.ndarray,
          target: dict[str, object],
          log_survival: np.ndarray,
          log_detection_mass: np.ndarray,
          detection_case: str,
      ) -> None:
          """Apply the inherited first-detection model in stable log arithmetic."""
          indices = np.flatnonzero(beached)
          if not len(indices) or when > target["date_end_value"]:
              return
          distance = d42core.haversine_grid_km(
              latitude[indices],
              longitude[indices],
              float(target["latitude_value"]),
              float(target["longitude_value"]),
          )
          if not np.isfinite(distance).all() or np.any(distance < 0.0):
              raise AssertionError("Recovery distances must be finite and nonnegative")
          log_kernel = -0.5 * np.square(distance / RECOVERY_BANDWIDTH_KM)
          central_mean_delay = 45.0 if bool(target["targeted_value"]) else 180.0
          mean_delay = central_mean_delay * DETECTION_CASES[detection_case]
          log_rate = log_kernel - math.log(mean_delay)
          log_hazard = log_hazard_from_log_rate(log_rate)
          if target["date_start_value"] <= when <= target["date_end_value"]:
              increment = log_survival[indices] + log_hazard
              log_detection_mass[indices] = np.logaddexp(
                  log_detection_mass[indices], increment
              )
          # 1 - hazard = exp(-rate), so log survival decreases by rate.  Rates below
          # the subnormal limit have no representable effect on survival, but their
          # log hazards above remain finite and are retained in detection mass.
          rate = np.zeros(log_rate.shape, dtype=float)
          representable = log_rate >= LOG_SMALLEST_SUBNORMAL
          rate[representable] = np.exp(log_rate[representable])
          log_survival[indices] -= rate


      def hybrid_distance_rank_strata(
          distance_km: np.ndarray,
          beached: np.ndarray,
          boundaries_km: np.ndarray,
          rank_bins: int,
      ) -> np.ndarray:
          """Combine fixed-distance enrichment with within-bin rank resolution."""
          distance = np.asarray(distance_km, dtype=float)
          beached = np.asarray(beached, dtype=bool)
          boundaries = np.asarray(boundaries_km, dtype=float)
          if distance.shape != beached.shape or distance.ndim != 1:
              raise ValueError("Distance and beach arrays must be equal vectors")
          if not np.isfinite(distance).all() or np.any(distance < 0.0):
              raise ValueError("Distances must be finite and nonnegative")
          if rank_bins < 1 or np.any(np.diff(boundaries) <= 0.0):
              raise ValueError("Hybrid-stratum parameters are invalid")
          fixed = np.searchsorted(boundaries, distance, side="right")
          fixed_count = len(boundaries) + 1
          labels = np.empty(len(distance), dtype=int)
          for beach_state in (False, True):
              for fixed_bin in range(fixed_count):
                  local = np.flatnonzero((beached == beach_state) & (fixed == fixed_bin))
                  if not len(local):
                      continue
                  rank = equal_count_rank_labels(distance[local], rank_bins)
                  labels[local] = rank + rank_bins * (
                      fixed_bin + fixed_count * int(beach_state)
                  )
          return labels


      def log_replicate_quality(
          log_values: np.ndarray,
          minimum_finite_replicates: int,
          maximum_coefficient_of_variation: float,
      ) -> dict[str, object]:
          """Evaluate replicate quality without exponentiating the absolute scale."""
          values = np.asarray(log_values, dtype=float)
          if values.ndim != 1 or len(values) < 2:
              raise ValueError("At least two one-dimensional replicates are required")
          if np.isnan(values).any() or np.isposinf(values).any() or np.any(values > 1e-12):
              raise ValueError("Log recovery probabilities must lie in [-inf, 0]")
          finite = np.isfinite(values)
          finite_count = int(np.count_nonzero(finite))
          if finite_count:
              maximum = float(np.max(values[finite]))
              scaled = np.zeros(len(values), dtype=float)
              scaled[finite] = np.exp(values[finite] - maximum)
              scaled_mean = float(scaled.mean())
              log_mean = maximum + math.log(scaled_mean)
              coefficient = float(scaled.std(ddof=1) / scaled_mean)
              log_standard_error = (
                  log_mean + math.log(coefficient) - 0.5 * math.log(len(values))
                  if coefficient > 0.0
                  else -math.inf
              )
          else:
              log_mean = -math.inf
              coefficient = math.inf
              log_standard_error = -math.inf
          passes = bool(
              finite_count >= minimum_finite_replicates

      for event_idx, observation in enumerate(observations):

              end = observation["date_end_value"]
              assert isinstance(start, date) and isinstance(end, date)
              if when > end:
                  continue

                  selected_latitude,
                  selected_longitude,
                  float(observation["latitude_value"]),
                  float(observation["longitude_value"]),
              )
              kernel = np.exp(-0.5 * (distance / BANDWIDTH_KM) ** 2)

      def step(
          latitude: np.ndarray,
          longitude: np.ndarray,
          beached: np.ndarray,
          lost: np.ndarray,
          when: date,
          current,
          wind,
          stokes,
          coast: CoastalMask,
          parameters: dict[str, np.ndarray],
          rng: np.random.Generator,
          coast_parameters: dict[str, float],
      ) -> tuple[int, int, int]:
          p_refloat = transition_probability(DT_DAYS, coast_parameters["lambda_refloat_days"])
          refloat = beached & (rng.random(beached.shape) < p_refloat)
          beached[refloat] = False

          moving = ~beached & ~lost
          if not np.any(moving):
              return int(refloat.sum()), 0, 0
          idx = np.flatnonzero(moving)
          flat_lat = latitude.flat[idx]
          flat_lon = longitude.flat[idx]
          current_u, current_v = current.velocity(when, flat_lat, flat_lon)
          wind_u, wind_v = wind.velocity(when, flat_lat, flat_lon)
          stokes_u, stokes_v = stokes.velocity(when, flat_lat, flat_lon)
          valid = (
              np.isfinite(current_u)
              & np.isfinite(current_v)
              & np.isfinite(wind_u)
              & np.isfinite(wind_v)
              & np.isfinite(stokes_u)
              & np.isfinite(stokes_v)
          )
          invalid_count = int(np.count_nonzero(~valid))
          if np.any(~valid):
              lost.flat[idx[~valid]] = True
          if not np.any(valid):
              return int(refloat.sum()), 0, invalid_count
          idx = idx[valid]
          old_lat = latitude.flat[idx].copy()
          old_lon = longitude.flat[idx].copy()
          extra_u, extra_v = constant_speed_left_of_wind(
              wind_u[valid],
              wind_v[valid],
              parameters["extra_speed_m_s"].flat[idx],
              parameters["left_angle_deg"].flat[idx],
          )
          east_velocity = (
              current_u[valid]
              + parameters["stokes_scale"].flat[idx] * stokes_u[valid]
              + parameters["direct_wind_fraction"].flat[idx] * wind_u[valid]
              + extra_u
          )
          north_velocity = (
              current_v[valid]
              + parameters["stokes_scale"].flat[idx] * stokes_v[valid]
              + parameters["direct_wind_fraction"].flat[idx] * wind_v[valid]
              + extra_v
          )
          sigma = math.sqrt(2.0 * DIFFUSIVITY_M2_S * DT_SECONDS)
          east_metres = east_velocity * DT_SECONDS + rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=len(idx))
          north_metres = north_velocity * DT_SECONDS + rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=len(idx))
          dlat, dlon = d42core.displacement_degrees(old_lat, east_metres, north_metres)
          candidate_lat = old_lat + dlat
          candidate_lon = old_lon + dlon
          in_domain = (
              (candidate_lat >= coast.land.latitude[0])
              & (candidate_lat <= coast.land.latitude[-1])
              & (candidate_lon >= coast.land.longitude[0])
              & (candidate_lon <= coast.land.longitude[-1])
          )
          if np.any(~in_domain):
              lost.flat[idx[~in_domain]] = True
          idx = idx[in_domain]
          old_lat = old_lat[in_domain]
          old_lon = old_lon[in_domain]
          candidate_lat = candidate_lat[in_domain]
          candidate_lon = candidate_lon[in_domain]
          if not len(idx):
              return int(refloat.sum()), 0, invalid_count

          midpoint_lat = 0.5 * (old_lat + candidate_lat)
          midpoint_lon = 0.5 * (old_lon + candidate_lon)
          shore_contact = coast.is_land(midpoint_lat, midpoint_lon) | coast.is_land(candidate_lat, candidate_lon)
          candidate_lat = np.where(shore_contact, old_lat, candidate_lat)
          candidate_lon = np.where(shore_contact, old_lon, candidate_lon)
          latitude.flat[idx] = candidate_lat
          longitude.flat[idx] = candidate_lon
          coastal = shore_contact | coast.is_coastal_ocean(candidate_lat, candidate_lon)
          p_beach = transition_probability(DT_DAYS, coast_parameters["lambda_beach_days"])
          newly_beached = coastal & (rng.random(len(idx)) < p_beach)
          beached.flat[idx[newly_beached]] = True
          return int(refloat.sum()), int(newly_beached.sum()), invalid_count

      The ordinary flaperon ensemble exports Kish effective sample size (ESS) for
      both the Réunion-arrival weights and the arrival-plus-isotope weights. A cell
      with an apparently sharp isotope density is not admitted when only a handful
      of paths carry its weight. Candidate cells are screened with a 100-fold guard
      band around the eventual 1% material-likelihood threshold, a minimum top-ten
      rule for every family/covariance case, and one-grid-step neighbor padding.

      A 1,024-particle × 4-replicate weighted screen identifies the final guarded
      set. Guarding is applied both to each family-conditional surface and to the
      pointwise equal-prior reference BMA; every BMA-material cell requires final
      weighted estimates from both current families. Those cells are then rerun with
      4,096 particles in each of eight independent
      replicates, resampled every 30 days across recovery-distance, beach-state, and
      partial-isotope-likelihood strata. Resampling preserves each occupied stratum’s
      total probability weight, and the full drift, detection-survival, and Kalman
      likelihood states share the same ancestry map. At every material cell, the median
      across independent replicates of particle-weight and root-lineage arrival ESS must
      both be at least 50; the two corresponding median arrival-plus-isotope ESS values
      must be at least 20. Root-lineage ESS first aggregates cloned descendants by their
      original trajectory and is therefore the conservative diversity diagnostic.
      Runtime invariants require its
      likelihood totals to equal the particle totals and its ESS not to exceed particle
      ESS. Pooled ESS is not substituted. Screening and final outputs remain separate.

      ## Predeclared checks

      1. WGS84 forward/inverse geodesic round trips must miss the requested offset by
         no more than 0.001 NM.
      2. Every zero-offset coordinate must equal the retained arc coordinate within
         `1e-12` degrees.
      3. Unit tests must confirm identical fixed random blocks for equal seeds and
         alpha-free raw score serialization.
      4. Production 8,192→16,384 nested convergence is reported separately for every
         current family and offset; no failed offset is hidden by marginalization.
      5. The 2D surface is not integrated over `n` without an explicitly named
         cross-arc measure or prior.
      6. If the final rare-event-corrected HYCOM, GDP, or equal-prior raw-likelihood
         BMA debris surface assigns more than 0.10 normalized mass to the two outer
         cross-arc columns, the strip is expanded symmetrically by 100 NM per side in
         25 NM steps and rerun until the gate passes or environmental support is
         reached. Along-arc endpoints are gated separately at the same 0.10 threshold
         and cannot be hidden by cross-arc expansion. The overlapping union of the
         two boundary sets is reported as a diagnostic; it is not imposed as a third,
         double-counting gate.
         In this check, and where the frozen policy artifact uses the legacy word
         “posterior,” normalized mass means the generalized compatibility surface
         normalized under the uniform strip-coordinate reference. It is not an
         ordinary crash-location posterior.
         Before mutation, the complete input and production state is copied into a
         hash-verified iteration archive. Previously computed cells are retained only
         after executable checks prove byte-identical numerical files, a configuration
         change limited to the expanded offset list, exact source-row preservation,
         and exact equality of any applicable target rows. Metadata is rebound to the
         expanded inputs with the predecessor hashes attached; the independent final
         audit rehashes every such reuse record.
      7. Every final rare-event log mean must be finite. Guarded family and reference-
         BMA material cells must also have at least four finite replicates and CV no
         greater than 2, and the normalized surfaces must pass the frozen replay/
         refinement convergence limits. Nonmaterial CV is exported for downstream
         re-guarding. No alpha, pseudocount, or floor is admitted into the point
         likelihood.
      8. The isotope term is admitted only if all 49 samples and three age maps pass
         chronology checks, the conditional-arrival estimator converges, and the
         substantive modes persist across the three covariance cases. Otherwise it
         remains a separately plotted sensitivity.

      ## Reproduction

      Use the repository's existing analysis virtual environment:

      ```bash
      D0060_PY=analyses/D-0010-wspr-trajectory-diagnostic/.venv/bin/python
      ```

      Run and audit the pilot. Do not launch production unless
      `outputs/pilot/pilot_audit.json` reports `production_launch_passes: true`.


      Run production, compose the ordinary diagnostic, then freeze and replace rare
      events:

      # D-0060 results — two-dimensional cross-arc debris and isotope likelihood

      Status: **IN PROGRESS — production and admission gates are still running**.

      ## Executive result

      `[UNKNOWN]` The final numerical modes, highest-density regions, family evidence
      update, and admission decisions will be inserted only from the completed generated
      artifacts. This report deliberately does not promote pilot values into production
      results.

      The estimand is a generalized conditional likelihood over a two-dimensional
      source strip, not an impact-location posterior. Fractional identity and
      site-dependence powers make it a generalized-Bayes compatibility factor, not a
      conventionally calibrated ordinary likelihood. An end-of-flight prior and any
      independent current-family validation evidence remain downstream inputs.

      ## Paper-ready summary

      Locations and recovery dates of debris reports spanning confirmed through
      unconfirmed 9M-MRO identity assessments can be combined with ocean data to ask
      which sources could have produced the finds. Identity class enters through
      predeclared fractional weights: D-0060 begins with the public 42-row inventory without
      treating every report as a certain, independent aircraft part. Five reports lack
      a usable date and one unofficial 2020 candidate exceeds common environmental
      support. The remaining 36 reports form 26 site-time events and receive
      predeclared weights for identity uncertainty and same-site dependence.

      Possible sources span a two-dimensional strip around the seventh satellite arc:
      25 latitude-labelled positions from 20°S to 44°S and nine geodesic offsets from
      -100 to +100 nautical miles. The arc is a coordinate reference, not an assumed
      endpoint. Across the grid, millions of objects are transported for each current
      family using daily HYCOM currents or a GDP drifter-based monthly climatology,
      with object-class leeway, wind and Stokes response, diffusion, stochastic
      beaching/refloating, coastal retention, detection delay, and reporting
      probability. Recovery means discovery/reporting, not the unknown beaching date:
      an object may beach and refloat repeatedly, and the likelihood includes survival
      against earlier detection.

      For current family `m`, along-arc coordinate `s`, cross-arc offset `n`, and debris
      evidence `D`, the calculation produces \(G_m(D\mid s,n)\), the raw generalized
      conditional likelihood—not a crash-location posterior or a conventionally
      calibrated ordinary likelihood. Ordinary Monte Carlo uses 16,384
      particles per cell with an 8,192-particle nested prefix for convergence. Very
      small probabilities are re-estimated with grouped weighted ensembles in natural-
      log space. This retains finite Gaussian tail likelihoods that would round to zero
      in ordinary binary64 arithmetic. Additive alpha, pseudocounts, and probability
      floors are forbidden: every canonical log mean must be finite, guarded material
      cells must pass the frozen replicate-precision gate, and the refined normalized
      surfaces must converge. Strong estimates that remain unstable are selected by a
      prospectively frozen, location-blind rule and rerun independently at 65,536×8;
      the replacement is never averaged with the estimate that triggered it, and a
      second failure stops admission rather than changing the threshold.

      The 49 nonblank measured rows in the A2-G1 flaperon barnacle dataset
      `[S-herbert-2023-mh370-barnacle-dataset-v4, Isotope_day2.csv, rows 2–50;
      SHA-256 5539161374130f143f6f822e658119ea0eb174e96fc27d4f3fc2991c926f4f29]`
      provide an optional late-path temperature mark; the source paper describes the
      record as covering only the final few months
      `[S-al-qattan-et-al-2023-barnacle-isotopes, p. 2, Introduction; author-PDF
      lines 134–144]`. The isotope density is conditioned on the already
      counted Réunion flaperon recovery, so that recovery is not counted twice.
      Three supplied shell-age chronology maps are marginalized with equal prior
      probability `[S-herbert-2023-mh370-barnacle-dataset-v4,
      Isotope_day_interpolate.csv; SHA-256
      bc9806f071e54697811ae3bf91c0ff481d598a5792d579bae03f9a1c8a32e194]`,
      and central, short-memory/tight, and long-memory/wide covariance cases test
      sensitivity to shared bias, path discrepancy, measurement error, and OISST
      uncertainty. Material cells are rerun with isotope-aware weighted ensembles and
      must pass effective-sample-size and sensitivity gates.

      HYCOM and GDP are reported separately. A reference combined chart uses an
      equal-prior generalized Bayesian model average of common-scale generalized
      likelihoods,
      \[
      G_{\mathrm{BMA}}(s,n)=\tfrac12G_{\mathrm{HYCOM}}(s,n)+\tfrac12G_{\mathrm{GDP}}(s,n),
      \]
      not the earlier predictive stacking weight. Because the debris terms use
      fractional identity and dependence powers, the update yields generalized family
      weights under the stated uniform strip-coordinate reference—not conventionally
      calibrated posterior model probabilities. Those weights are not transferred into
      a flight analysis. The integrated estimator combines each admitted raw family
      generalized likelihood with an independent end-of-flight prior and an
      independently justified current-family prior on the exported native source grid.

      `[UNKNOWN — final production modes, highest-density regions, generalized family
      weights, and admission decisions will be inserted from generated artifacts.]`
      The final figures report the two-dimensional density mode, along-arc marginal mode, and
      cross-arc marginal mode separately, and compare the result with published drift
      regions only as context because those studies use different evidence and
      conditioning.

      ## What is estimated

      For along-arc coordinate `s`, signed cross-arc offset `n`, current family `m`,
      and the retained debris evidence `D`, D-0060 estimates

      ```math
      L_m(D\mid s,n).
      ```

      The optional A2-G1 barnacle record `I` enters once as a conditional mark on the
      already-counted Réunion flaperon recovery `R_f`:

      ```math
      L_m(D\mid s,n)\,p(I\mid R_f,s,n,m).
      ```

      Positive `n` points toward the nominal Inmarsat-3F1 sub-satellite point; negative
      `n` points away. The grid contains 25 one-degree latitude-labelled arc bins from
      20–44°S and nine WGS84 geodesic offsets from -100 to +100 NM in 25 NM steps.
      The seventh arc is a coordinate reference, not a prior ridge and not a claim that
      impact on the curve itself is especially likely. The frozen WGS84 audit reports
      a maximum offset error of `2.86 × 10^-9 NM`, exact zero-offset coordinates, and
      a nondegenerate one-sided tangent at the southern endpoint
      `[DERIVED: outputs/source_grid_audit.json]`. Trapezoidal quadrature gives boundary
      cells half the cross-arc width of interior cells.

      ## Debris evidence and timing

      `[S-al-qattan-et-al-2023-barnacle-isotopes, p. 9, section 3.4; author-PDF
      lines 486–490]`. Colonization timing is represented only through the three
      supplied shell-age maps. Component-specific colonization, growth, mortality,
      seawater-δ18O, or older-barnacle data could support future likelihood factors if
      their acquisition and calibration were available; they are not invented here.

      ## Reading the maps and modes

      The maps normalize each family-conditional surface, and a separate reference
      HYCOM–GDP generalized Bayesian model average, under a named uniform density in along-arc
      chainage and cross-arc distance. Fill is displayed on a logarithmic scale and is
      clipped only for display at `10^-4` of each panel peak. The 50%, 75%, and 95%
      lines are highest-density regions of the discrete conditional surface.

      Kadri, Godfrey/WSPR, and the UGIB 2020 comparator are deliberately absent from
      the production charts. The published-location supplement is limited to the
      remaining documented debris-drift and explicitly conditioned context estimates;
      none is statistically pooled with D-0060.

      Three different summaries must not be conflated:

      - the **2D density mode** is the highest-density source cell;
      - the **along-arc marginal density mode** is found by summing cell mass over
        offsets and dividing out the along-coordinate quadrature width;
      - the **cross-arc marginal density mode** is found by summing over arc bins and
        dividing out the cross-coordinate quadrature width.

      The division matters at the half-width boundary bins; selecting by bin mass
      alone would not be a coordinate-density mode.

      The final report table will present all three. A separated northern or southern
      region can be material even when it is not the 2D global mode.

      ## Combining HYCOM and GDP

      The reference combined plot is the equal-prior generalized Bayesian model average

      ```math
      G_{\mathrm{BMA}}(s,n)=\tfrac12 G_{\mathrm{HYCOM}}(s,n)
                               +\tfrac12 G_{\mathrm{GDP}}(s,n).
      ```

      The pointwise raw generalized likelihoods are combined before any strip
      normalization; the method does not average two separately normalized family maps.
      Equal family priors therefore do not force HYCOM and GDP to contribute half of the
      final normalized mass. A weaker-evidence family contributes less through the
      resulting generalized-evidence update. Thus a northern GDP feature can survive
      only when its raw likelihood is competitive at those cells, not because GDP was
      assigned half of a normalized-map pool. This is not the earlier predictive
      stacking method. The resulting generalized family weights are coherent for the
      declared fractional likelihood, but are not conventionally calibrated ordinary
      posterior model probabilities. They are not copied into the integrated
      end-of-flight estimator.

      ## Sensitivity to fractional-likelihood scale

      `[ASSUMPTION]` The primary D-0060 result uses debris learning rate
      \(\eta=1\). This is the predeclared fractional-likelihood scale, not an
      estimate that calibrates ordinary posterior probabilities. Because source mass
      and generalized family weights can depend on this scale, a frozen audit also
      evaluates \(\eta=0.5,0.75,1,1.5,2\). For debris-only surfaces it uses

      ```math
      \ell_{m,\eta}^{D}=\eta\log G_m(D\mid s,n),
      ```

      and for isotope-augmented surfaces it uses

      ```math
      \ell_{m,\eta}^{D,I}=\eta\log G_m(D\mid s,n)
      +\log p(I\mid R_f,s,n,m).
      ```

      Thus the proper conditional isotope density remains at exponent one. Tempering
      the complete isotope-augmented log column would answer a different question.
      The generated audit reports family weights, modes, northern/southern band mass,
      boundary mass, total variation, and Hellinger distance from the primary
      \(\eta=1\) surface. It is sensitivity-only and cannot change an admission
      decision. A complete calibrated latent identity/dependence model remains the
      stronger route to ordinary posterior source and current-family probabilities.


      ## Production result tables

      `[UNKNOWN — awaiting completed production surfaces]`

      the flight prior is applied, any newly generalized-posterior-material cell with a nonzero
      exported rare-replicate precision-failure count must be refined before the
      integrated generalized posterior is finalized.

      ## Relation to published drift studies

      Nominal item counts are not directly comparable because studies use different
      identity thresholds and evidence units. The audited public corpus reports: Nesterov
      (2018) aggregated 27 “possibly relevant and confirmed” fragments by region
      `[S-nesterov-2018-mh370-debris-drift, p. 11; chunk
      S-nesterov-2018-mh370-debris-drift:p0011:c01]`; Ulich et al. (2020) used 20
      likely-or-higher item reports including co-located dependencies
      `[S-ulich-godfrey-iannello-banks-2020-final-resting-place, p. 176; chunk
      S-ulich-godfrey-iannello-banks-2020-final-resting-place:p0176:c01]`; Durgadoo
      et al. (2021) used nine site representatives
      `[S-strategies-simulating-marine-debris-drift-2021, p. 9; chunks
      S-strategies-simulating-marine-debris-drift-2021:p0009:c02–c03]`; and Ulich
      and Iannello (2023) used 17 de-duplicated high-probability sites
      `[S-ulich-et-al-2023-drift-study, p. 71; chunk
      S-ulich-et-al-2023-drift-study:p0071:c01]`.

      `[SOURCE]` Their reported location summaries also have different conditioning:
      Nesterov reports 25.5–30.5°S with a 28–30°S core
      `[S-nesterov-2018-mh370-debris-drift, p. 1; chunk
      S-nesterov-2018-mh370-debris-drift:p0001:c01]`; ATSB/CSIRO reports 32–36°S,
      near 35°S, after also considering surface search and Australian non-find evidence
      `[S-atsb-2016-first-principles-review, p. 18; chunk
      S-atsb-2016-first-principles-review:p0018:c03]`; Griffin/CSIRO reports 35.6°S,
      92.8°E conditional on some Pleiades objects being aircraft debris
      `[S-csiro-2017-ocean-drift-part-iii, p. 6; chunk
      S-csiro-2017-ocean-drift-part-iii:p0006:c01]`; and Miron et al. report a
      combined maximum near 31°S with single-find groups near 25°S and 36°S
      `[S-markov-chain-inspired-search-mh370-2019, p. 6; chunk
      S-markov-chain-inspired-search-mh370-2019:p0006:c03]`. The Ulich and Iannello
      debris-only result peaks at 34.0°S with significant probability over 30–36°S
      `[S-ulich-et-al-2023-drift-study, p. 6; chunk
      S-ulich-et-al-2023-drift-study:p0006:c02]`.

      `[DERIVED]` D-0060 has a larger nominal dated-report set (36), but handles
      identity and same-site dependence with fractional/capped weights rather than
      claiming 36 independent recoveries. Differences in current products, retained
      evidence units, windage/Stokes treatment, coastal retention, detection/reporting,
      rare-event estimation, and the inclusion of search, image, or route evidence can
      all move a reported mode. None of those published studies supplies a common-scale
      two-family raw likelihood and full covariance information that can simply be
      inserted as another equal-prior Bayesian family. Their published regions remain
      contextual comparisons, not additional likelihood terms.

      Passage locators and hashes are in
      `knowledge/citations/recovered-debris-drift-coverage-ledger.md` and
      `knowledge/citations/D-0055-published-location-comparison-ledger.md`.

      ## Limitations

      - Current-field, non-flaperon leeway, beach/refloat, detection, and reporting errors
        are structural assumptions, not ordinary Monte Carlo uncertainty.
      - The coast grid cannot resolve individual beaches, surf-zone transport, burial,
        removal, fragmentation, waterlogging, or changing observer effort.
      - Fractional identity/event weights are a declared generalized likelihood, not a
        fitted generative identity model.
      - OISST and HYCOM are not acquisition-independent because both assimilate overlapping
        satellite and in-situ information.
      - A conditional compatibility surface is not a calibrated crash-location posterior.
